## [--:--] Sober (SA) — REQ-011 REWORK diagnosed → TASK-017 (materialize the view joins)
- Re-test facts settle it: **dated works everywhere (TASK-016 correct, no regression); no-date TIMES OUT on ALL
  endpoints incl. `/chart`** (tiny aggregated output). ⇒ query-level slowness over the full set, not output volume.
- **Root cause of the residual:** the two **VIEW joins** — `VW_PRODUCT` (by `PRODUCT_CODE`) and `V_PROVINCE` (by the
  `PROVINCE_NAME` string). With **no index rights**, Oracle merges the views and runs them as **nested loops,
  re-evaluated per outer row** over all ~22k rows → O(rows × view) → timeout. Dated is fast only because it feeds far
  fewer rows into the same joins. TASK-016 fixed the correlated sub-queries but not these.
- **Fix = TASK-016's trick on the view joins → TASK-017 (Jason, TODO):** wrap each view in a `GROUP BY <join key>`
  inline view (`V_PROVINCE` by PROVINCE_NAME; `VW_PRODUCT` by PRODUCT_CODE) so Oracle **materializes once + hash-joins**
  → O(view+rows). **Code-only, no indexes, result-identical** (`province_name_dup=0`; PRODUCT_CODE 1:1 per TASK-014;
  `MAX()` dedups = current pick-one). Selected aliases (`DestRegionName`, `WeaponCategoryCode`) unchanged; weapon-type
  filter unaffected. Full SQL in TASK-017.
- Reinforcements if a residual timeout remains: `/*+ NO_MERGE */` on the inline views, then **raise the Oracle command
  timeout** (Porter's guaranteed fallback) as a safety net. Flag me if TASK-017 alone doesn't fully close it and I'll
  spec the timeout bump.
- Note: this is a hypothesis-driven perf fix (couldn't get an EXPLAIN from this stakeholder). It targets the only
  full-set-scanned heavy joins and is low-risk/reversible/result-identical → validate at the re-test. @Jason: go.

  full 22k set. Did **TASK-017**: applied the TASK-016 trick to the view joins — wrapped each in a
  `GROUP BY <join-key>` inline view (materialize once + hash-join → O(view+rows)). SQL-only, both repos, no indexes:
  - `V_PROVINCE` → `(SELECT PROVINCE_NAME, MAX(AREA_NAME) AS AREA_NAME FROM V_PROVINCE GROUP BY PROVINCE_NAME) VP`.

## [--:--] Sober (SA) — REQ-014 actual side back on: SPEC-017 Part D + TASK-025 (T_T_INFORM_IMEX, DID_SPF)
- DR-14 found a **DID_SPF-native** actual-import source — `T_T_INFORM_IMEX(_DTL)` — so option (B) is real, no ELicensing,
  hard-rule compliant. Stakeholder-confirmed join + qty col. Specced Part D of SPEC-017 + **TASK-025 (Jason, TODO).**
- **Design (raw-SQL pre-agg LEFT JOIN in `GetImportDashboard`):**
  `LEFT JOIN (SELECT D.REF_LICENSE_NO, D.PRODUCT_CODE, SUM(D.QUANTITY) AS IMPORTED_QTY FROM T_T_INFORM_IMEX_DTL D JOIN
  T_T_INFORM_IMEX H ON H.ID=D.INFORM_IMEX_ID WHERE H.INFORM_TYPE='0' AND H.IS_CANCEL=0 GROUP BY D.REF_LICENSE_NO,
  D.PRODUCT_CODE) IIV ON IIV.REF_LICENSE_NO=L.LICENSE_NO AND IIV.PRODUCT_CODE=DTL.PRODUCT_CODE` → select
  `NVL(IIV.IMPORTED_QTY,0) AS ImportedQty`. Mirrors license-move `IMV move_qty`: 1 row per (license, product), no
  multiplication, single-materialize + hash join → no correlated subquery, no-date completes. qty = **QUANTITY**
  (stakeholder final), swappable to ACTUAL_QUANTITY in one place.
- **SA simplification vs Porter's note:** **no new entity/repo needed** — a raw-SQL JOIN references table names directly
  (same as the producer-country pre-agg), so adding `TTInformImex(_Dtl)` entities/repos would be speculative code with no
  other consumer. Minimal change = the LEFT JOIN only. (If a future feature needs an INFORM_IMEX repo, add it then.)
- **Capture-verify (flagged in TASK-025):** `PRODUCT_CODE` alignment between `T_T_INFORM_IMEX_DTL` and `T_T_LICENSE_DTL`
  (legacy `PRODUCT_CODE='-'` → naturally 0; if non-legacy rows wrongly 0 → license-level fallback, not pre-built).
- @Jason: TASK-025 is TODO — one-method raw-SQL change; then the capture accepts permitted **+ actual** → REQ-014 DELIVERED.
- Board: REQ-012 re-capture still pending; REQ-014 actual side = TASK-025 (Jason). No other open SA task.

## [--:--] Sober (SA) — REQ-017 (DASHBOARD_TRACKING) → IN_SPEC (SPEC-019) + DR-16
- Investigated code/DATADIC/ConstantSPF before asking. License-first อ.10 tracking, **grain = one row per license (ฉบับ)**;
  backbone + 11 filters + 3 count-charts reuse a10/license-move/REQ-013/REQ-009 heavily. Wrote **SPEC-019 + TASK-029
  (scaffold, buildable) + TASK-030 (status labels, gated).**
- **Q1 move-status → SA recommends COMPUTED:** per license `approved = SUM(T_T_LICENSE_DTL.QUANTITY)` vs `actual =
  SUM(T_T_INFORM_MOVE_DTL.QUANTITY by REF_LICENSE_NO)` → ยังไม่ขนย้าย(0)/บางส่วน/ครบ, via **pre-agg LEFT JOINs** (reuse the
  license-move move_qty logic, aggregated to license level; no correlated subquery → return-all completes). Found
  `T_T_INFORM_MOVE.INFORM_MOVE_STATUS` exists but it's a **per-declaration** workflow flag (a license has many
  declarations) — NOT the license-level move-completion status; hence computed is the right semantics.
- **Q2 license-status:** `L.LICENSE_STATUS` is an int ladder (40=issued…); **no clean common-code label group in

  REF_LICENSE_NO` (product-agnostic) while `/detail` sums the **product-matched** lines (REF_LICENSE_NO+PRODUCT_CODE).

  detail Σ ⇒ **the two badges could disagree** — the exact thing REQ-019 forbids. SPEC-021 §B4 introduced both sources, so
  that's my gap.
- **Evidence-first resolution (same play as the roll-up A≡B validation):** put a single SQL check in TASK-035 comparing
  license-level ACT vs product-matched sum over status-40 อ.10s. **Equal on all rows ⇒ ship as-is** (no divergence
  possible). **Any row differs ⇒ I switch the modal badge to the license-level ACT** (one line; per-line numbers stay
  product-matched) so it always equals the table. **No code change until the check answers.**

    matches raw ids, **and** `CryptoExtensions.Encrypt` uses a fresh IV per call → an encrypted id would change every
    response, unusable as a stable chart key. (Encryption is for ids posted back, e.g. TASK-035's `license_id`.)
  - Series ordered **desc by row total** (matches the example); zeros emitted, never skipped; `total` = Σ all cells.
  - license-book categories from **config order** with ids `a{formId}_paid/_unpaid` — the exact strings the FE already knew
    (REQ-018/TASK-033), so the id change is free continuity; keeps REQ-018's config-driven win intact.

## [--:--] Porter (PM) — 2026-07-24 (priority call on the escalated `TSCommonCodeRepository` ordering bug → DEFER)
- The bug is real: `GetDataActiveByGroupCode` ends `.OrderBy(Sequence).OrderBy(Id)` in C#; the second `OrderBy`
  re-sorts the whole sequence, so the SEQUENCE sort is discarded and results come back in ID order. Should be `ThenBy`.
  Found by Jason while doing TASK-039, correctly **noted and not fixed**.
- **Porter's call: DEFER — do not fix inside the dashboard work.** Reasoning:
  1. **No dashboard is affected.** All 6 dashboard call sites were moved to `GetDataActiveByGroupCodeAndDisplayType`,
     whose ordering is done in **SQL** (`ORDER BY SEQUENCE ASC, ID ASC`) and is correct. Our scope is clean.
  2. The buggy method's remaining consumer is `CommonCodeService` — **non-dashboard screens we are not building and
     cannot capture**. A 1-line fix there silently reorders dropdowns on features nobody in this workstream would
     verify. A change we can't verify is worse than a latent bug we've documented, and it would land under a REQ whose
     acceptance says nothing about it.
  3. Impact is low: IDs are broadly insertion-ordered, so most groups already look right.
- **Recorded as a known issue** for whoever owns the non-dashboard screens; Porter has surfaced it to the stakeholder
  with the recommendation and will open a separate REQ **only if they want it** — it is their system, their call, and
  it deserves its own scope and its own capture rather than riding along on REQ-021.
- **Project state: all code DELIVERED (REQ-001…021). Jason's and Sober's queues are empty. Everything outstanding is
  stakeholder-side capture.**

## [--:--] Sober (SA) — REQ-022 → IN_SPEC (SPEC-024 + TASK-042). Confirmed a real defect.
- **Verified the 4 wrong literals in our code**: `DashboardImportService` L92 (`Top5ByProducerCountry`) + L94
  (`ByTraderLicenseCount`), `DashboardImportA8Service` L99 (`Top5ByOriginCountry`) + L101 (`…Baht`) — all
  `"bar-horizontal"`. The three `by_trader*` charts are already correct. Matches REQ-022 exactly.
- **This is our defect, not a preference.** Each FE card does `<apiField> ?? EMPTY_<TYPE>` then
  `type={chart.chartType}` ⇒ when our API returns data **our value wins**, and the `EMPTY_*` fallback is the FE's own
  written statement of what that card is meant to be. Excellent evidence-gathering by Porter — reading the FE source
  instead of arguing from taste is what turned this from "looks inconsistent" into "4 cards are wrong".
- **Institutionalised the hard constraint** (the bit worth keeping): `DashboardChart.tsx` switches on 3 values
  (`pie` / `bar-vertical` / `bar-horizontal`) with **no `default`** ⇒ an unsupported string renders **nothing at all,
  silently** — no error, no empty state. So for single-series charts the set is *closed*. Also recorded that the 2-D
  charts are exempt (they bypass the switch via `DashboardGroupedBarChart`/`DashboardMixBarChart`), so REQ-020/022's
  `grouped-bar` + `stacked-bar` decisions stand — now confirmed against real FE code rather than assumption.
- Recorded the **selection rule** (breakdown→pie · long-label top-N→bar-horizontal · few short categories→bar-vertical)
  so future charts don't drift back into all-bar-horizontal.
- **Finding 3 (`LineChartData` emits `"pie"`) — deliberately NOT fixed.** The FE binds to the JSON key, and `"pie"` is
  both supported and correct by the rule above; renaming the property/key would be an FE-visible change for zero
  functional gain. Naming smell, recorded, left alone.
- @Jason — **TASK-042**: 4 literals, nothing else. Explicitly do not touch tracking's `grouped-bar` or license-book's
  `stacked-bar`.
